The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`.\n- `order([\"a\"], [[\"a\",\"a\"]])` throws `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n- With `nodes [\"a\",\"b\",\"c\",\"d\"]` and edges `[[\"d\",\"b\"],[\"c\",\"d\"]]`, the result is `[\"a\",\"c\",\"d\",\"b\"]`.\n"}],"timestamp":1794047337769}}

Unknown node check — before any ordering work, even when cyclic.\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    if (!nodeSet.has(before)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${before}`);\n    if (!nodeSet.has(after)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${after}`);\n  }\n\n  // Build adjacency (dependencies) and count of prerequisites.\n  // Duplicate edges impose the same single constraint, so skip repeats.\n  const deps = new Map(); // id -> set of ids that must come before it\n  for (const node of nodeSet) deps.set(node, new Set());\n  const seenEdges = new Set();\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    const key = `${before}\\u0000${after}`;\n    if (seenEdges.has(key)) continue;\n    seenEdges.add(key);\n    deps.get(after).add(before);\n  }\n\n  const orderByEdges = [...nodeSet].sort();\n  const remaining = new Map();\n  for (const node of orderByEdges) remaining.set(node, new Set(deps.get(node)));\n\n  const result = [];\n  const emitted = new Set();\n\n  while (result.length < orderByEdges.length) {\n    // Find the alphabetically smallest node whose prerequisites are emitted.\n    let next = null;\n    for (const node of orderByEdges) {\n      if (emitted.has(node)) continue;\n      let block = -1;\n      for (const prereq of remaining.get(node)) {\n        if (!emitted.has(prereq)) {\n          block = 1;\n          break;\n        }\n      }\n      if (block === -1) {\n        next = node;\n        break;\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (next === null) {\n      // Cycle: no node is ready. {"type":"tool_execution_end","toolCallId":"call_efa93f97a8004327806ad7f0","toolName":"read","result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"export function order(nodes, edges) {\n  // Treat nodes as a set (duplicates are not an error).\n  const nodeSet = new Set(nodes);\n\n  // 1. Unknown node check — before any ordering work, even when cyclic.\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    if (!nodeSet.has(before)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${before}`);\n    if (!nodeSet.has(after)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${after}`);\n  }\n\n  // Build adjacency (dependencies) and count of prerequisites.\n  // Duplicate edges impose the same single constraint, so skip repeats.\n  const deps = new Map(); // id -> set of ids that must come before it\n  for (const node of nodeSet) deps.set(node, new Set());\n  const seenEdges = new Set();\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    const key = `${before}\\u0000${after}`;\n    if (seenEdges.has(key)) continue;\n    seenEdges.add(key);\n    deps.get(after).add(before);\n  }\n\n  const orderByEdges = [...nodeSet].sort();\n  const remaining = new Map();\n  for (const node of orderByEdges) remaining.set(node, new Set(deps.get(node)));\n\n  const result = [];\n  const emitted = new Set();\n\n  while (result.length < orderByEdges.length) {\n    // Find the alphabetically smallest node whose prerequisites are emitted.\n    let next = null;\n    for (const node of orderByEdges) {\n      if (emitted.has(node)) continue;\n      let block = -1;\n      for (const prereq of remaining.get(node)) {\n        if (!emitted.has(prereq)) {\n          block = 1;\n          break;\n        }\n      }\n      if (block === -1) {\n        next = node;\n        break;\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (next === null) {\n      // Cycle: no node is ready. Trace a real cycle through unemitted nodes.\n      const unemitted = orderByEdges.filter((n) => !emitted.has(n));\n      const start = unemitted[0];\n      const path = [start];\n      const pos = new Map([[start, 0]]);\n      let cur = start;\n      // Follow edges among unemitted nodes until a node repeats.\n      for (;;) {\n        const curDeps = deps.get(cur);\n        // Among unemitted, find any node that depends on cur via a real edge.\n        let nextNode = null;\n        for (const node of unemitted) {\n          if (emitted.has(node)) continue;\n          if (remaining.get(node).has(cur)) {\n            nextNode = node;\n            break;\n          }\n        }\n        if (nextNode === null) {\n          // Shouldn't happen in a cycle; but guard.\n          break;\n        }\n        if (pos.has(nextNode)) {\n          // cycle from pos.get(nextNode) to end\n          const cycle = path.slice(pos.get(nextNode)).concat(nextNode);\n          throw new Error(`cycle detected: ${cycle.join(\" -> \")}`);\n        }\n        pos.set(nextNode, path.length);\n        path.push(nextNode);\n        cur = nextNode;\n      }\n      throw new Error(\"cycle detected\");\n    }\n\n    emitted.add(next);\n    result.push(next);\n  }\n\n  return result;\n}"}]},"isError":false}

The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`.\n- `order([\"a\"], [[\"a\",\"a\"]])` throws `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n- With `nodes [\"a\",\"b\",\"c\",\"d\"]` and edges `[[\"d\",\"b\"],[\"c\",\"d\"]]`, the result is `[\"a\",\"c\",\"d\",\"b\"]`.\n"}],"timestamp":1794047337769},{"role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Let me look at the existing structure first."},{"type":"toolCall","id":"call_2caf82389eb847ea892b7b61","name":"bash","arguments":{"command":"ls -la src/ 2>/dev/null;
